These observations do not identify a precise renderer bottleneck or prove that DCE4 is the only progressing guest event.

This comparison rules out the prior DCF4 stack sample as a universal explanation for the slow title-event-enabled run. It supports that event-shadow waits can return and the GPU queue can drain while rendering remains very slow. ### 2026-10-20 XBE title-poller trace

Continued the title-input investigation with the generated XBE translation and the XBE's embedded switch-table bytes. The title loop calls `sub_0004AA80` to refresh input, then `sub_000C8050` and `sub_000C8080` to query controls 9 and 8 in input group 6. The switch table resolves both calls to `sub_00048B80`, which reads the low 16-bit values at guest addresses `0x4AFC08` and `0x4AFC04`, respectively. `sub_00048890` copies these values from the companion state area at `0x4B0160` and `0x4B015C`.

A bounded, scheduler-enabled run used the real-DSP Release executable, Steel Battalion USB profile, and no synthetic DSP acknowledgement. The Main Weapon A press was consumed by the host live-input path at about 68.7 seconds. A low-overhead `ReadProcessMemory` sample read the mapped aliases for guest `0x4AFC04` and `0x4AFC08` (runtime mapping offset `0x10000`) 137 times across 15 seconds; both remained zero. Renderer counters reached 305 flips and fell to about 0.1 FPS. This run did not save a visual capture proving the title prompt was present at the time of the press, and it did not pair that press with a USB payload/callback trace. It therefore narrows the state boundary but does not establish that the game rejected input.

Static review of `sub_00049970` shows that the game refreshes device state through an indirect device interface; `sub_0004AA80` then copies/transforms that state before the title queries it. Earlier OHCI tracing verified that the Main Weapon bit reached the active guest DMA buffer and that the guest claimed write-done interrupts during startup. It did not establish that the transfer-completion handler copies the report into the game-owned device state or that this interface feeds the two title-query slots. The next trace should follow the XAPI transfer-completion path through `sub_00049970` / `sub_00048890` to the title query, then repeat only with a visible prompt and simultaneous report/state sampling. Keep the 305/318-flip presentation stall as a separate investigation.

### 2026-10-20 Steel Battalion XID protocol cross-check

Compared the current virtual device profile with public XID references. The profile advertises Steel Battalion type/subtype `0x80/0x01` with 26-byte input and 22-byte output reports, matching the [Xbox Input Devices reference](https://xboxdevwiki.net/Xbox_Input_Devices). That reference identifies `RightJoyMainWeapon` at input byte 2, bit 0, consistent with the captured `00 1A 01` report during the live A hold. An independent [XboxDev/NXDK Steel Battalion probe](https://gist.github.com/Ryzee119/5b74e94eb765bcc54e68afde9ddc7623) uses a packed 26-byte struct whose analog fields start at byte 8, while the wiki table places them one byte later. Because the public offsets conflict, no analog mapping change was retained; analog input remains unverified pending a stronger reference or a hardware capture.

The protocol reference also documents a 22-byte Xbox-to-controller report. The current OHCI runtime accepts and discards endpoint OUT data, so controller indicator/output behavior is not implemented. The opt-in virtual XID device remains a test profile, not physical-controller support. No code or copyrighted game data was copied from the references. A normal Release rebuild completed after reverting the unverified analog-offset experiment; no game process remains. Milestone 3 and progress bars remain unchanged.

### 2026-10-20 USB DPC trace and missing guest callback seed

A bounded trace at the linked XboxRecomp kernel bridge confirms the OHCI ISR queues guest DPC `0x0025C308` with context `0x01081A18`; the registered ISR is `0x0025BD47`. Static XBE translation shows the DPC dispatches completed OHCI work and can reach callbacks through the controller stack. The earlier run failed 970 indirect calls to guest function `0x0025A3D7`, which was missing from the build's active `xboxrecomp-generated-fixed` dispatch table. A local-only seed for that existing XBE function translation was added to the ignored generated tree, then the x64 Release executable compiled and linked. A matched 24-second startup run continued to operational OHCI and claimed interrupts, with no `0x0025A3D7` unresolved-call report. The diagnostic process was stopped after the bounded window.

This verifies the import-to-DPC handoff, identifies one missing translated callback, and removes that specific unresolved call in the sampled run. It does not verify that the controller report reaches the title's input state, that the title prompt advances, or that audio/gameplay is functional; no title-screen success is claimed from this short run. The generated seed and all game-derived C remain local/ignored and were not committed. Temporary trace changes to the linked XboxRecomp kernel bridge were restored byte-for-byte; the ordinary release target was rebuilt successfully. Logs are in `iso/local-analysis/logs/m3-dpc-handoff-20261020/`. Progress bars remain unchanged; Milestone 3 stays active.

### 2026-10-20 reproducible XBE seed regeneration

The tracked `analysis/steel-battalion-xbe-function-seeds.json` already contained `0x0025A3D7`, but did not mark it as a runtime-observed address. Added the observation and its source note so XboxRecomp's seed loader can trust the measured indirect-call entry even when its previous linear sweep treated it as an interior boundary. Re-ran `tools.disasm` against the supplied XBE with `--seed-functions`; it recovered exactly one function at `0x0025A3D7–0x0025A52B` (340 bytes, 91 instructions). `tools.func_id` and `tools.abi_analysis` completed, then `tools.recomp --all --split 1000` translated 9,379 functions with zero translation failures. The new dispatch contains `0x0025A3D7`, and the generated unresolved stubs do not.

Configured the local Release build to use that fresh ignored output and successfully rebuilt the Windows target. In a matched 24-second USB startup run, OHCI became operational after about 5.1 seconds and claimed periodic interrupts; the run had no unresolved-call message for `0x0025A3D7`. Logs and all generated code remain local/ignored under `iso/local-analysis/xboxrecomp-dpc-seeded/`. The tracked seed manifest contains only addresses and analysis notes; no XBE, assets, or generated game code is committed.

This resolves reproducibility for that callback and the specific unresolved-call symptom. It does not prove game-side input state updates, title prompt acceptance, stable rendering, audio, or campaign play. The run stopped at the 24-second bound. The generated set still reports 100 other unresolved indirect targets and 243 unimplemented instruction sites overall. Milestone 3 and progress bars remain unchanged.

## 2026-10-20 XID dispatch and contiguous-page collision follow-up
- Added observed XBE callback seed `0x0025D059` to `analysis/steel-battalion-xbe-function-seeds.json`; the existing `0x0025D090` seed was merged into the runtime generation set. v6 disassembly recognized both boundaries, and XboxRecomp generated 9,403 functions with zero translation failures. Runtime traces now enter `sub_0025D059` instead of logging it as an unresolved indirect call.
- Reviewed XboxRecomp upstream commit [4f3307f](https://github.com/sp00nznet/xboxrecomp/commit/4f3307f3692065ecb6ffd7b0e62bf21d97da6dcf), which diagnoses the same physical-address ambiguity between a separate contiguous window and XBE image pages preventing XPP/OHCI enumeration. Ported its opt-in behavior locally and recorded it in `patches/xboxrecomp-v0.13.1-steel-battalion-contig-avoid-image.patch`. The diagnostic log confirmed a contiguous allocation overlapping XBE pages `0x00012000..0x00904E48` was moved beyond the image when `RECOMP_CONTIG_AVOID_IMAGE=1`.
- In the patched USB run, OHCI reported up to 2,743 periodic transfers and 2,609 reports, and the renderer reached 171 flips / roughly 14 FPS before presentation stopped. A follow-up also enabled the available upstream guest-CPU lock (4 ms quantum); it reached 165 flips / roughly 14.6 FPS before stopping. Both captures remained black after the opening logos and did not reach the title prompt. No title input was sent. Neither run verifies guest acceptance, audible sound, or gameplay.
- The first v6 run's per-call trace output overwhelmed the diagnostic; later runs used counting-only tracing. Each stalled diagnostic process was stopped, and no game process remains.
- This narrows the startup problem: the callback dispatch and an XPP/OHCI memory collision are addressed, but a later black-frame/presentation stall remains. Do not advance Milestone 3 or project progress bars. Next compare the stalled guest wait/stream path against the earlier title-reaching capture, keeping USB enabled, and sample the guest thread state at the freeze before changing behavior further.

## 2026-10-20 upstream APU fix cross-check
- Reviewed the current upstream XboxRecomp release and merged [PR #205](https://github.com/sp00nznet/xboxrecomp/pull/205). Its measured Black-specific DirectSound stall was caused by the APU sample-limit interrupt and `XGSCNT` frequency: the title programmed SSL, reset the counter, enabled ISTS bit 3, and waited for its tick. Upstream changed the counter to 48 kHz and raises that interrupt. This is a credible lead, but the evidence is from Black and does not show Steel Battalion uses the same path.
- The local XboxRecomp v0.13.1 runtime still returns virtual-clock nanoseconds divided by 100 for `XGSCNT`, not a 48 kHz sample count. A bounded Steel Battalion audio-trace launch reached title-audio asset loading (through `SNK_009.wav`) but produced no `[APUMMIO]` trace entries and then exited on an access violation reading guest VA `0xFFFFFFF4` in the DSOUND code path. Because the trace failed to show register traffic and the crash was not reproduced, neither result confirms the SSL hypothesis or a general startup crash.
- No APU change was made. The targeted run did not verify audible output or reach the title prompt; no game process remains. The x64 MSVC Release toolchain is already installed and used for prior successful builds, so no Visual Studio installation was needed. Progress bars and Milestone 3 remain unchanged.
- Next verify why `RECOMP_APU_TRACE` emitted no register records, then determine from Steel Battalion's own APU writes whether it programs SSL/enables ISTS bit 3. Only if that path is observed should the narrowly scoped upstream fix be adapted and retested.
